Review report: the places the migration was not certain
======================================================================

Page boundaries placed on differing text: 1380 of 56182 (2.46%)
Page markers placed on differing text:    2168 of 158365 (1.37%)

A boundary decides where a page begins, so those are given in full.
A marker only decides where a small page-number badge sits, so those
are summarised by book.

For each case: what the original TPR text has at that point, then what
sits at the position chosen in ePitaka. If the two read the same, the
placement is right even though the words around it differ.


PAGE BOUNDARIES
======================================================================

        original: nissaya vipāka āhāra indriya atthi avigatanti | nahetuyā ekaṃ naārammaṇe ekaṃ naanantare ekaṃ
        placed:   nahetuyā ekaṃ, naārammaṇe ekaṃ, naanantare ekaṃ, nasamanantare | ekaṃ, naaññamaññe ekaṃ, naupanissaye ekaṃ, napurejāte ekaṃ,
  mula_bi_07_04             1  (thai 1)
      e.g. T4.451
        original: naupanissayapaccayā tīṇi naāsevanapaccayā suddhake arūpamissake ca | vipāka nti niyāmetabbaṃ rūpamissake natthi saṃkhittaṃ
        placed:   Naupanissayapaccayā … tīṇi … naāsevanapaccayā (suddhake | arūpamissake ca ‘‘ vipākan ’’ ti niyāmetabbaṃ,
  mula_ku_11                1  (myanmar 1)

  mula_ku_14                1  (thai 1)

  mula_ku_20                1  (myanmar 1)

  mula_sa_01                1  (vri 1)
      e.g. V1.2

PAGE NUMBERING THAT DOES NOT SIMPLY ASCEND
======================================================================

Repeats are usually the same page anchored twice around a heading, and
are handled by taking the first. Restarts are a second printed volume
inside one TPR book. Steps backwards are the ones worth a look.
